- **baa-boundary-reasoning**: Activate when the BAA Boundary Reasoner Agent runs or when a regulatory inquiry hits multiple affected provider customers. Teaches how to reason about a single regulatory inquiry against multiple differing BAA terms. ([mlbrilliance/cascadecare-network-command](https://github.com/mlbrilliance/cascadecare-network-command/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/baa-boundary-reasoning))
- **baa-ch01-introduction-to-agents**: Scoping guidance for agentic systems, based on Chapter 1 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Covers the real-agent test, choosing code vs workflow vs RAG vs agent, async "work arrives prepared" design, agent types and reference use cases, model selection and routing, LangGraph/AutoGen/CrewAI/OpenAI Agents SDK trade-offs, five build principles, and organizing teams. Use when someone asks whether a problem needs an agent, which model or framework to start with, how to make an agent scalable and maintainable, or how to govern agent experiments. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch01-introduction-to-agents))
- **baa-ch02-designing-agent-systems**: Guides end-to-end design of an agent system, based on Chapter 2 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers scoping a first bounded workflow, model selection (size, modality, open vs proprietary, routing, VRAM), tools, memory, orchestration, speed/scale/reliability/cost trade-offs, single vs multiagent choice, evaluation and phased rollout. Use when starting an agent project, choosing or pricing models, deciding whether to go multiagent, or planning agent testing and production launch. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch02-designing-agent-systems))
- **baa-ch03-ux-design-for-agentic-systems**: UX design guidance for AI agents: choosing and combining modalities (text, GUI, generative UI, voice, video), autonomy sliders, sync vs async flows, proactive notifications, context and personalization, capability discovery, confidence signaling, clarifying questions, graceful failure and trust. Based on Chapter 3 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Use when designing or reviewing an agent's user-facing interaction, picking a modality, setting human-approval levels, writing error or clarification flows, or diagnosing low adoption or trust. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch03-ux-design-for-agentic-systems))
- **baa-ch04-tool-use**: Guidance for designing, choosing, securing and operating the tools an AI agent calls, based on Chapter 4 ("Tool Use") of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers local, API, plug-in and MCP tools, tool metadata, stateful-tool safety, agent-generated tools and tool-choice/fallback control. Use when defining or reviewing tool schemas, deciding where a tool should live, exposing a database or API to an agent, adopting MCP, letting an agent write code, or hardening tool-call failures. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch04-tool-use))
- **baa-ch05-orchestration**: Guidance for orchestrating AI agents, based on Chapter 5 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Covers agent archetypes (reflex, ReAct, planner-executor, query decomposition, reflection, deep research), standard/semantic/hierarchical tool selection, tool parametrization and execution, tool topologies (single, parallel, chain, graph) and context engineering. Use when choosing an agent type, picking tools from a large catalog, designing a chain or LangGraph-style graph, assembling per-step context, or evaluating planning approaches. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch05-orchestration))
- **baa-ch06-knowledge-and-memory**: Guides the design of agent knowledge and memory, based on Chapter 6 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers context-window budgeting, rolling FIFO windows, BM25 keyword memory, vector-store semantic memory, RAG, semantic experience memory, GraphRAG and knowledge-graph construction, long context vs retrieval, dynamic graphs, and self-notes. Use when choosing or debugging an agent's memory or retrieval layer, deciding whether GraphRAG is worth it, planning a context budget, or hardening a knowledge graph. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch06-knowledge-and-memory))
- **baa-ch07-learning-in-agentic-systems**: Guidance for making agents improve from experience, based on Chapter 7 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Covers nonparametric learning (dynamic few-shot exemplars, Reflexion, ExpeL-style insight lists) and parametric learning (when to fine-tune, SFT for function calling, DPO, RLVR, small vs large models). Use when deciding whether an agent should learn at all, designing a feedback or memory loop, choosing between prompting and fine-tuning, preparing tool-call or preference datasets, or picking a model size to tune. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch07-learning-in-agentic-systems))
- **baa-ch08-from-one-agent-to-many**: Guidance for deciding when to move from one agent to many, how to split responsibilities, which coordination pattern to use (democratic, manager, hierarchical, swarm, actor-critic, automated design/ADAS), and which communication, execution and state infrastructure fits (A2A, Kafka/Redis/NATS, Ray/Orleans/Akka, Temporal/Airflow). Based on Chapter 8 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Use when an agent has too many tools, when designing a supervisor or multiagent topology, when adding an agent, or when choosing brokers, actors, workflow engines or persistence for agents. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch08-from-one-agent-to-many))
- **baa-ch09-validation-and-measurement**: Evaluation and validation playbook for LLM agents, based on Chapter 9 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Covers hero scenarios and metric choice, structured eval cases and how to grow them, unit tests for tools, planning, memory and learning, end-to-end scoring (tool recall/precision, parameter accuracy, phrase recall, task success), consistency, coherence, hallucination, hostile inputs, and release gates. Use when designing an agent eval suite, scoring agent traces, debugging regressions, or deciding if an agent is ready to ship. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch09-validation-and-measurement))
- **baa-ch10-monitoring-in-production**: Production monitoring for LLM agents, based on Chapter 10 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers failure-versus-noise triage, a layered metric taxonomy, stack choice (Grafana/Loki/Tempo, ELK, Phoenix, SigNoz, Langfuse), OpenTelemetry span design, dashboards and alerts, shadow and canary releases, regression traces, self-healing fallbacks, user feedback, drift tests (KS, KL, PSI) and RACI ownership. Use when instrumenting, observing, alerting on, rolling out or debugging deployed agents. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch10-monitoring-in-production))
- **baa-ch11-improvement-loops**: Designs feedback-driven improvement loops for production agent systems, based on Chapter 11 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Covers failure detection and root cause analysis, HITL escalation thresholds, prompt and tool refinement (DSPy, Trace, APO), backlog prioritization, shadow deployments, A/B tests, Bayesian Bandits, in-context adaptation and offline retraining. Use when an agent keeps failing in production, when planning how to validate or roll out a prompt/tool change, when sizing or reading an experiment, or when deciding how fixes should persist. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch11-improvement-loops))
- **baa-ch12-protecting-agentic-systems**: Security and resilience guidance for LLM agent systems, based on Chapter 12 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers agent-specific risks, HITL weaknesses, prompt-injection threat taxonomy, model selection and layered defenses, red teaming, MAESTRO threat modeling, data privacy/provenance, agent safeguards, external threats and internal failures. Use when designing, reviewing, threat-modeling, red-teaming, hardening or monitoring an agent, its tools, data flows or multiagent setup. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch12-protecting-agentic-systems))
- **baa-ch13-human-agent-collaboration**: Human-agent collaboration guidance based on Chapter 13 of Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents". Covers human roles (executor to governor), progressive delegation, adoption rollouts, agent scopes and RBAC, scoped memory, trust repair, accountability (audits, logs, traceability), escalation thresholds, and compliance in CI. Use when deciding how much autonomy an agent gets, designing approval or handoff flows, planning a pilot, scoping memory or permissions for team or enterprise agents, or preparing governance and regulatory controls. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-ch13-human-agent-collaboration))
- **baa-complete**: Whole-book guide to Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" for designing, building, evaluating, operating and securing agentic and multiagent systems end to end. Use when a task spans several stages or it is unclear which chapter applies. Routes to per-topic references from all 13 chapters and bundles 15 tested scripts (tool linting, topology checks, context budgets, eval reports, trace and drift analysis, A/B and bandit analysis, permission audits). ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-complete))
- **baa-playbook**: Draft business associate agreements and subcontractor BAAs when a BAA matter arrives, producing a complete markdown agreement with timing placeholders and defaults for missing facts. - **baa-theme-evaluation-and-operations**: Theme skill on measuring, operating and improving agents in production, from Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (chapters 9 to 11). - **baa-theme-memory-and-learning**: Theme skill on how agents remember and improve, from Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (chapters 6 and 7). Use when designing context windows and memory stores, choosing keyword, vector, RAG, experience memory or GraphRAG, building knowledge graphs, weighing long context against retrieval, or adding learning loops (dynamic few-shot, Reflexion, experiential rules, SFT, DPO, RLVR). ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-theme-memory-and-learning))
- **baa-theme-security-and-governance**: Theme skill on protecting agentic systems and governing human-agent work, from Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (chapters 12 and 13). Use when threat modeling an agent (MAESTRO), defending against prompt injection and data leaks, auditing tool permissions, red teaming, designing safeguards and incident response, or planning autonomy levels, scope-based access, memory governance, escalation, accountability and compliance. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-theme-security-and-governance))
- **baa-theme-tools-orchestration-multiagent**: Theme skill on how agents act and coordinate, from Michael Albada's "Building Applications with AI Agents" (chapters 4, 5 and 8). Use when defining or auditing tools (local, API, MCP, stateful), choosing tool selection and agent archetypes (ReAct, planner-executor, reflection), designing chains and graphs, doing context engineering, or deciding whether and how to split into multiple agents and wire their communication and state. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/building-applications-with-ai-agents-albada/baa-theme-tools-orchestration-multiagent))
